Following the [2024 Venezuelan presidential elections](60825396.md), Panama's Foreign Ministry issued a statement together with the foreign ministers of Argentina, Costa Rica, Ecuador, Paraguay, Peru and Uruguay, requesting guarantees that the government would respect the election results.[^cite_note-\:15-171] The following day, on 29 July, president Mulino announced the suspension of relations between both countries after rejecting and denouncing irregularities regarding the announcement of Maduro's presidential victory.[^cite_note-222] On 29 July Maduro's government ordered the expulsion from the country of the diplomatic staff of the Panamanian Embassy in Venezuela.[^cite_note-\:20-176] The [Socialist International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_International) condemned the expulsion of the Panamanian diplomatic corps.[^cite_note-\:21-177]

On 2 August 2024 president Mulino announced that Panama was joining the recognition of [Edmundo González Urrutia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmundo_Gonz%C3%A1lez_Urrutia) as president-elect. Following the ruling of the [TSJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Tribunal_of_Justice_of_Venezuela) on 22 August endorsing Nicolás Maduro's victory for his third term, the governments of Argentina, Chile, Costa Rica, Ecuador, the United States, Guatemala, Panama, Paraguay, Peru, the Dominican Republic and Uruguay issued a statement the following day rejecting the ruling.[^cite_note-\:29-179]

#### Paraguay

On 10 January 2019 the government of [Mario Abdo Benítez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Abdo_Ben%C3%ADtez) broke [relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paraguay-Venezuela_relations) with Venezuela and closed the Paraguayan embassy in Caracas.[^cite_note-\:93-182] Relations were restored in November 2023 following the [arrival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Santiago_Pe%C3%B1a?action=edit\&redlink=1) of [Santiago Peña](58414156.md).[^cite_note-\:82-223]

Following the 2024 presidential elections, Paraguay's Foreign Ministry issued a statement together with the foreign ministers of Argentina, Costa Rica, Ecuador, Panama, Peru and Uruguay, requesting guarantees that the government would respect the election results.[^cite_note-\:15-171]

#### Peru

The [government of Nicolás Maduro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_government_of_Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro?action=edit\&redlink=1) and the [government of Dina Boluarte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Dina_Boluarte?action=edit\&redlink=1) experienced diplomatic friction after the [National Police of Peru](14896441.md) injured members of Venezuela's national football team during the match between both countries held in Lima in 2023[^cite_note-224] and the Venezuelan team's aircraft was denied fuel for several hours without any apparent reason.[^cite_note-225]

Following the presidential elections, Peru's Foreign Ministry issued a statement together with the foreign ministers of Argentina, Costa Rica, Ecuador, Panama, Paraguay and Uruguay, requesting guarantees that the government would respect the election results.[^cite_note-\:15-171]

After the results of the 2024 presidential elections, Peruvian foreign minister [Javier González-Olaechea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javier_Gonz%C3%A1lez-Olaechea?action=edit\&redlink=1) declared that Peru "will not accept the violation of the popular will of the Venezuelan people", rejecting the results.[^cite_note-\:18-205] On 29 July Maduro's government ordered the expulsion from the country of the diplomatic staff of the Peruvian Embassy in Venezuela.[^cite_note-\:20-176] The [Socialist International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_International) condemned the expulsion of the Peruvian diplomatic corps.[^cite_note-\:21-177] Following the results published by the Democratic Unitary Platform, which contradicted those of the [CNE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Electoral_Council_\(Venezuela\)), Peru recognised [Edmundo González Urrutia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmundo_Gonz%C3%A1lez_Urrutia) as president-elect. Following the ruling of the [TSJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Tribunal_of_Justice_of_Venezuela) on 22 August endorsing Nicolás Maduro's victory for his third term, the governments of Argentina, Chile, Costa Rica, Ecuador, the United States, Guatemala, Panama, Paraguay, Peru, the Dominican Republic and Uruguay issued a statement the following day rejecting the ruling.[^cite_note-\:29-179]

#### Dominican Republic

Following the [2024 presidential elections](60825396.md), the Dominican Foreign Ministry joined a statement together with the foreign ministers of Argentina, Costa Rica, Ecuador, Panama, Paraguay, Peru and Uruguay, requesting guarantees that the government would respect the election results.[^cite_note-226] On 29 July Maduro's government ordered the expulsion from the country of the diplomatic staff of the Dominican Embassy in Venezuela.[^cite_note-\:20-176] The [Socialist International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_International) condemned the expulsion of the Dominican diplomatic corps.[^cite_note-\:21-177] Following the ruling of the [TSJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Tribunal_of_Justice_of_Venezuela) on 22 August endorsing Nicolás Maduro's victory for his third term, the governments of Argentina, Chile, Costa Rica, Ecuador, the United States, Guatemala, Panama, Paraguay, Peru, the Dominican Republic and Uruguay issued a statement the following day rejecting the ruling.[^cite_note-\:29-179]

#### United States of America

See also: [Operation Money Badger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Money_Badger) and [Proposed United States invasion of Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposed_United_States_invasion_of_Venezuela)

Maduro accused the [United States of intervention in Venezuela](2071275.md) several times with his allegations ranging from post-election violence by "[neo-Nazi](54361.md) groups", economic difficulties from what he called an "economic war" and various coup plots.[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-NPR8march-228] The United States denied such accusations.[^cite_note-NPR8march-228]



Maduro meeting with U.S. Secretary of State [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry), 26 September 2016

In early 2015 the Obama administration signed an [executive order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_executive_actions_by_Barack_Obama) which imposed targeted sanctions on seven Venezuelan officials whom the White House argued were instrumental in human rights violations, persecution of political opponents and significant public corruption and said that the country posed an "unusual and extraordinary threat to the national security and [foreign policy of the United States](7564.md)".[^cite_note-229] Maduro responded to the sanctions in a couple of ways. He wrote an open letter in a full-page ad in *The New York Times* in March 2015, stating that Venezuelans were "friends of the American people" and called President Obama's action of making targeted sanctions on the alleged human rights abusers a "unilateral and aggressive measure".[^cite_note-FOXson-230][^cite_note-231] Examples of accusations of human rights abuses from the United States to Maduro's government included the murder of a political activist prior to legislative elections in Venezuela.[^cite_note-232] Maduro threatened to sue the United States over an executive order issued by the Obama Administration that declared Venezuela to be a threat to American security.[^cite_note-233] He also planned to deliver 10 million signatures, or signatures from about 1/3 of Venezuela's population, denouncing the United States' decree declaring the situation in Venezuela an "extraordinary threat to US national security".[^cite_note-234][^cite_note-GPsanctions-235] and ordered all schools in the country to hold an "anti-imperialist day" against the United States with the day's activities including the "collection of the signatures of the students, and teaching, administrative, maintenance and cooking personnel".[^cite_note-GPsanctions-235] Maduro further ordered state workers to apply their signatures in protest, with some workers reporting that firings of state workers occurred due to their rejection of signing the executive order protesting the "Obama decree".[^cite_note-GPsanctions-235][^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239][^cite_note-240] There were also reports that members of [Venezuelan armed forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_armed_forces) and their families were ordered to sign against the United States decree.[^cite_note-GPsanctions-235]

##### Proposed United States invasion of Venezuela

These paragraphs are an excerpt from [Proposed United States invasion of Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposed_United_States_invasion_of_Venezuela).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Proposed_United_States_invasion_of_Venezuela\&action=edit)]

The possibility of a [United States](3434750.md)-led invasion of [Venezuela](32374.md) has been frequently discussed during the presidencies of [Donald Trump](4848272.md).

In 2017, during his [first presidency](52231341.md), Trump said that he was "not going to rule out a military option" to address the worsening [crisis in Venezuela](52966258.md). In 2025, during his [second presidency](78298108.md), the [United States surged military assets to the Caribbean](80987786.md) as part of [Operation Southern Spear](81593523.md), citing the fight against [drug cartels](197015.md) as its stated objective. During this buildup, experts, analysts and current and former government officials stated that the assets deployed were insufficient for an invasion and that they viewed an invasion as unlikely.

In 2026, the United States launched [Operation Absolute Resolve](82021123.md), conducting [airstrikes](323167.md) on Venezuela and capturing President [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) and his wife [Cilia Flores](9530834.md). This led to questions being raised about the possibility of long-term military intervention involving ground troops in Venezuela.

#### Uruguay

On 13 February 2024 president Luis Lacalle Pou said that it was "obvious" that in Venezuela "there is a dictatorship".[^cite_note-241] On 17 February former president [José Mujica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mujica) declared: "The [government of Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_government_of_Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro?action=edit\&redlink=1) is an [authoritarian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism), (Maduro) can be called a dictator".[^cite_note-242]

Following the [2024 presidential elections](60825396.md), Uruguay's Foreign Ministry issued a statement together with the foreign ministers of Argentina, Costa Rica, Ecuador, Panama, Paraguay and Peru, requesting guarantees that the government would respect the election results.[^cite_note-\:15-171] Following the results, president [Luis Lacalle Pou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Lacalle_Pou) declared that "The process up to election day and the counting process were clearly tainted. One cannot recognise a victory if there is no confidence in the methods and mechanisms used to achieve it" and that he would not recognise the CNE results declaring Nicolás Maduro the winner.[^cite_note-\:18-205] On 29 July Maduro's government ordered the expulsion from the country of the diplomatic staff of the Uruguayan Embassy in Venezuela.[^cite_note-\:20-176] The [Socialist International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_International) condemned the events.[^cite_note-\:21-177] On 2 August Lacalle Pou's government recognised [Edmundo González Urrutia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmundo_Gonz%C3%A1lez_Urrutia) as president-elect. Following the ruling of the [TSJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Tribunal_of_Justice_of_Venezuela) on 22 August endorsing Nicolás Maduro's victory for his third term, the governments of Argentina, Chile, Costa Rica, Ecuador, the United States, Guatemala, Panama, Paraguay, Peru, the Dominican Republic and Uruguay issued a statement the following day rejecting the ruling.[^cite_note-\:29-179]

### Europe

#### Spain

The Spanish government recognised [Juan Guaidó](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Guaid%C3%B3) as interim president of Venezuela in 2019, leaving the position of ambassador to Venezuela vacant from 2020 until 2022, when a new ambassador was appointed.[^cite_note-\:34-243]

#### France

Nicolás Maduro concluded his 2017 international tour in [France](5843419.md), meeting with president [François Hollande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Hollande).[^cite_note-244]

#### Italy

Following the 2024 presidential elections in Venezuela, Italian foreign minister [Antonio Tajani](1767298.md) declared: "I have many doubts about the regular conduct of the elections in Venezuela", requesting "results that can be verified".[^cite_note-\:16-245]

#### Portugal

On 30 March, at 00\:45 hours (Venezuelan time), the Venezuelan coast guard vessel [AB Naiguatá (GC-23)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AB_Naiguat%C3%A1_\(GC-23\)?action=edit\&redlink=1) sighted the Portuguese cruise ship *Resolute* and ordered it to follow it to a Venezuelan port. The captain of the *Resolute* was consulting with his superiors when the *Naiguatá* deliberately collided with the cruise ship's bow with the apparent intention of diverting its course toward the Venezuelan coast. However, the *Resolute* was a ship with a reinforced hull designed for navigation in ice fields, causing the *Naiguatá* to take on water and eventually sink. Its crew was rescued by Venezuelan vessels, and the *Resolute*, which was not significantly damaged, after waiting in the area received instructions to head to [Curaçao](65205.md).[^cite_note-sit_well-116]

The *Resolute*, with its 32 crew members, had already been adrift for a day.[^cite_note-sit_well-116]

The government of [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) (partially recognised) denounced the incident, calling it an "act of international piracy",[^cite_note-246] while the [Bolivarian National Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolivarian_National_Armed_Forces) likewise described the Portuguese cruise ship's "action" as "cowardly and criminal".[^cite_note-247] The government of [Portugal](23033.md) offered to assist in the investigations.[^cite_note-248]

On 1 April, [Curaçao](65205.md) reported that it had not had access to the *Resolute*.[^cite_note-249] On 2 April, [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) stated that "someone from the north" was preventing Curaçao from investigating what had happened, in reference to the [United States](3434750.md).[^cite_note-250]

#### Russia



Nicolás Maduro and [Vladimir Putin](32817.md).

In 2015 Russian companies began leading international oil extraction in Venezuela, surpassing China.[^cite_note-\:33-193]

In 2017 the Russian state-owned company [Rosneft](1313566.md) paid Venezuela an advance of 6 billion dollars for oil sales.[^cite_note-\:33-193]

By 2020 the channel [CNBC](223441.md) considered Russia Venezuela's principal geopolitical ally, and Russia's "most prominent" Latin American relationship was with Venezuela.[^cite_note-CNBC-251]

Due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), an agreement was reached with Russia at the end of December 2020 for the delivery of 10 million units of Russian vaccines, beginning with 1.43 million first doses; by August 2021 the second dose had not arrived,[^cite_note-252] despite the fact that the second dose was supposed to be administered after 21 days. Russia responded that it first had to meet the needs of the Russian population, which totalled 140 million people.[^cite_note-253]

In February 2022 Nicolás Maduro expressed his "strong support" for Russia in its [invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) during a telephone call with [Vladimir Putin](32817.md)[^cite_note-254] and condemned the sanctions imposed on Russia by Western nations.[^cite_note-255]

### Asia

#### China

Maduro reached out to [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Venezuela_relations) for economic assistance while China funneled billions of dollars from multiple loans into Venezuela.[^cite_note-IBT2014-256] China is Venezuela's second largest trade partner with two-thirds of Venezuelan exports to China composed of oil.[^cite_note-IBT2014-256] According to Mark Jones, a Latin American expert of the [Baker Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_A._Baker_III_Institute_for_Public_Policy), China was "investing for strategic reasons" rather than ideological similarities.[^cite_note-IBT2014-256] The Venezuelan military also used military equipment from China using the [NORINCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NORINCO) [VN-4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VN-4) armoured vehicle against protesters during the 2014–15 Venezuelan protests, ordering hundreds more as a result of the demonstrations.[^cite_note-Janes-257][^cite_note-AR2014-258]

#### Iran



Nicolás Maduro and Hassan Rouhani.

Since 2020, during the [fuel crisis in Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuel_shortages_in_Venezuela), the government resorted to importing oil and refining supplies from Iran.[^cite_note-259] Through an agreement, Maduro's government handed over the [El Palito Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Palito_Refinery?action=edit\&redlink=1) to Iran for the maintenance and restoration of production at the plant in May 2022.[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] In June of that year, Nicolás Maduro and Ebrahim Raisi signed a 20-year cooperation agreement between [Venezuela](32374.md) and [Iran](14653.md).[^cite_note-262] At the end of July, it was reported that the government ceded one million hectares of arable land to the Iranian government.[^cite_note-263] According to the constitution, this decision could not be implemented without consulting the population, in accordance with Article 13.[^cite_note-264]

Iranian foreign minister [Nasser Kanaani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_Kanaani) recognised Nicolás Maduro as the winner following the [2024 presidential elections](60825396.md), congratulating him.[^cite_note-\:31-214]

#### Israel and Palestine

In the [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md), Maduro frequently supported the Palestinian cause in international forums including his stance that his country recognizes Jerusalem as the eternal capital of Palestine after the US embassy move to Jerusalem which he called an "extremist decision" that lacks legal validity and violates international law.[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268] In January 2019, Maduro reaffirms the unconditional support for the struggle of the Palestinians in defense of their sovereignty in accordance with the principles of the Charter of the United Nations and international law.[^cite_note-269]

In December 2017, Maduro was invited as the special honorary guest at the [Extraordinary Summit of the OIC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sixth_Extraordinary_Session_of_the_Islamic_Summit_Conference) ([Organisation of Islamic Cooperation](47725.md)) in [Istanbul](3391396.md), Turkey with the main issue being to unify the response of the [Muslim world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_world) to the US embassy move to Jerusalem, which Maduro called a "colonial act."[^cite_note-nyt2-270]

#### Syria

Nicolás Maduro expressed support for the government of [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md) in [Syria](7515849.md) on numerous occasions, opposing intervention in the [country](30741795.md).[^cite_note-271]

### International organizations

#### UN



UN High Commissioner for Human Rights [Michelle Bachelet](986814.md) led an [investigation into Maduro's government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachelet_report?action=edit\&redlink=1) that led to the dismantling of the [FAES](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Action_Forces).

Main article: [Independent International Fact-Finding Mission on Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_International_Fact-Finding_Mission_on_Venezuela)

#### BRICS

The government of Nicolás Maduro intended for Venezuela to join the [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS).[^cite_note-272] In 2024 Brazil vetoed Venezuela's entry into BRICS after Venezuela refused to publish electoral tally sheets from the 2024 elections amid opposition allegations of electoral fraud. This was criticised by Maduro's government.[^cite_note-273]

#### OAS

Minutes after Nicolás Maduro was sworn in, the [Organization of American States](22788.md), in an extraordinary meeting of its [Permanent Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_Council_of_the_Organization_of_American_States), approved a resolution declaring Maduro illegitimate as president of Venezuela and urging that new elections be called.[^cite_note-274]

#### Declaration of Panama

On 6 April 2015, twenty-five (25) ex-presidents issued a *Declaración de Panamá* (Declaration of Panama),[^cite_note-275] a statement denouncing the *VII Cumbre de las Américas*, what they called "democratic alteration" in Venezuela, promoted by the government of Nicolas Maduro. The statement calls for the immediate release of "political prisoners" in Venezuela. Among the former heads of government that have called for improvements in Venezuela are: [Jorge Quiroga](344089.md) (Bolivia); [Sebastián Piñera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebasti%C3%A1n_Pi%C3%B1era) (Chile): [Andrés Pastrana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9s_Pastrana), [Álvaro Uribe](243680.md) and [Belisario Betancur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belisario_Betancur) (Colombia); [Miguel Ángel Rodríguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_%C3%81ngel_Rodr%C3%ADguez), [Rafael Ángel Calderón Guardia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_%C3%81ngel_Calder%C3%B3n_Guardia), [Laura Chinchilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Chinchilla), [Óscar Arias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%93scar_Arias), [Luis Alberto Monge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Alberto_Monge) (Costa Rica), [Osvaldo Hurtado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osvaldo_Hurtado) (Ecuador); [Alfredo Cristiani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfredo_Cristiani) and [Armando Calderón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armando_Calder%C3%B3n?action=edit\&redlink=1) (EL Salvador); [José María Aznar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mar%C3%ADa_Aznar) (Spain); [Felipe Calderón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felipe_Calder%C3%B3n) and [Vicente Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicente_Fox) (México), [Mireya Moscoso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mireya_Moscoso) (Panamá), [Alejandro Toledo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alejandro_Toledo) (Perú) and [Luis Alberto Lacalle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Alberto_Lacalle) (Uruguay).[^cite_note-276]

#### Non-Aligned Movement

At the 17th Summit of the [Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement) (NAM) in 2016, hosted by Venezuela at [Margarita Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margarita_Island), Maduro was elected chairperson by acclamation, a position that he is to hold until the 18th Summit in Azerbaijan in 2019.[^cite_note-277] Maduro's administration spent over US\$120 million on the event,[^cite_note-DWNAM-278] and Maduro billed it as a meeting that would "be remembered for centuries"; according to Al Jazeera, the "delegates who did come complained privately of a lack of organisation, delays and shabby hotels".[^cite_note-AJNAM-279] The Maduro administration did not respond to a request from *Al Jazeera* for a list of delegations present;[^cite_note-AJNAM-279] of the 120 NAM member states, media sources estimated between 10[^cite_note-FPNAM-280] and 15[^cite_note-281] heads of state attended, including Bolivia,[^cite_note-AJNAM-279] Cuba, Ecuador, Palestine, Iran, Syria and Zimbabwe.[^cite_note-FPNAM-280] For only the second time since NAM was founded, India did not attend;[^cite_note-FPNAM-280] of the countries that did attend, many are recipients of Venezuelan oil subsidies, according to *Foreign Policy* and Fox News.[^cite_note-FPNAM-280][^cite_note-282]

#### International Criminal Court investigation

This section is an excerpt from [International Criminal Court investigation in Venezuela](62526848.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Venezuela\&action=edit)]

An investigation by the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) (ICC) to analyze possible [crimes against humanity](61099.md) committed in [Venezuela](32374.md) was opened in 2021.[^cite_note-International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Venezuela_\:1-283] A preliminary examination was initially opened in 2006, and closed after concluding that the requirements to start an investigation had not been met.[^cite_note-International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Venezuela_ICC_letter_VE_9Feb2006-284] In February 2018, the ICC announced that it would open preliminary probes into alleged crimes against humanity performed by Venezuelan authorities since at least April 2017.[^cite_note-285] In 2020, the Office of the Prosecutor of the ICC stated that it believed there was a "reasonable basis" to believe that "since at least April 2017, civilian authorities, members of the armed forces and pro-government individuals have committed the crimes against humanity",[^cite_note-International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Venezuela_\:0-286] and on 2021 ICC Prosecutor [Karim Khan](11606957.md) announced the opening of an investigation regarding the situation in the country.[^cite_note-International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Venezuela_\:1-283]

## Opposition

### 2014–present: Venezuelan protests

Main article: [2013–2026 Venezuelan protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protests_against_Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro)

Since 2014, a series of protests, political demonstrations, and civil insurrection began in Venezuela due to the country's high levels of violence, inflation, and chronic shortages of basic goods[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342] attributed to economic policies such as strict price controls.[^cite_note-economist.com-56][^cite_note-343] Maduro's government saw the protests as an undemocratic [coup d'etat](25914154.md) attempt[^cite_note-Kurmanaev-344] orchestrated by "[fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascist) opposition leaders and the [United States](3434750.md)".[^cite_note-Milne-345]

> If they want to march every day, go ahead and march ... I will use the [iron fist](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/iron%20fist) granted to me by [Chávez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Ch%C3%A1vez). Make no mistake about me. I am willing to do anything to defend the homeland, its sovereignty and our people.

— Nicolás Maduro

[^cite_note-346]

Although Maduro, a former [trade union](17626.md) leader, says he supports peaceful protesting,[^cite_note-comision-347] the Venezuelan government was widely condemned for its handling of the protests. Venezuelan authorities have reportedly gone beyond the use of rubber pellets and tear gas to instances of live ammunition use and torture of arrested protestors, according to organizations like [Amnesty International](18947898.md)[^cite_note-AIabuses-348] and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md),[^cite_note-HRWpfp-349] while the [United Nations](31769.md)[^cite_note-ENoct8-350][^cite_note-ENoct12-351][^cite_note-UN22oct-352] accused the Venezuelan government of [politically motivated](37307.md) arrests, most notably former [Chacao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chacao,_Venezuela) mayor and leader of [Popular Will](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_Will), [Leopoldo Lopez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopoldo_Lopez), who used the controversial charges of murder and inciting violence against him to protest the government's "[criminalization of dissent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminalization_of_dissent)."[^cite_note-NYTlopez-353][^cite_note-reuters_arrests-354][^cite_note-355]

Protests dwindled through 2015 and into 2016, though a movement to recall Maduro rekindled anti-government sentiment among Venezuelans, culminating with over one million protesting nationwide on September 1, 2016.[^cite_note-BBC_March_2016-356][^cite_note-Reuters_Revocalo_2016-357] Protests since then have continued, especially due to controversies surrounding the recall movement and the continued socioeconomic hardships Venezuelans face on a daily basis.

### Recall referendum project

Main article: [2016 Venezuelan recall referendum project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Venezuelan_recall_referendum_project)

The process to hold a recall referendum to vote on recalling Maduro was started on May 2, 2016. On that date, opposition leaders in Venezuela handed in a petition to the [National Electoral Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Electoral_Council_of_Venezuela) (CNE) that started a several stage process.[^cite_note-BBC_June2016_referendum-358] As of July 2016, the Venezuelan government had stated that if enough signatures were collected, a recall vote would be held no sooner than 2017.[^cite_note-Reuters_Recall_August2016-359]



The blue line represents percentage that favor recalling President Maduro. The red line represents percentage that do not wish to recall President Maduro. Unfilled dots represent individual results of the polls. Most polls have been discontinued due to the suspension of the recall movement.

#### Initial petition

On May 2, 2016, opposition leaders in Venezuela handed in a petition calling for a recall referendum. On June 21, 2016, the [BBC](19344654.md) reported that signatures for a referendum to recall Maduro were being recorded by the [National Electoral Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Electoral_Council_\(Venezuela\)) (CNE), with the process ongoing for several days. The petition required 1% of the electorate to endorse it before the next stage of voting could be held.[^cite_note-BBC_June2016_referendum-358] According to opposition leaders, in July during a preliminary signature drive for the recall, the CNE "rejected more than half a million signatures for reasons ranging from unclear handwriting to smudged fingerprints."[^cite_note-Reuters_Ellsworth_2016-360]

In early July 2016, Barack Obama urged Venezuela to allow the recall referendum.[^cite_note-WSJ_Ouster_-361] On July 5, 2016, the Venezuelan intelligence service detained five opposition activists involved with the recall referendum, with two other activists of the same party, [Popular Will](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_Will), also arrested.[^cite_note-WSJ_Ouster_-361]

According to a July 27, 2016 article in *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)*, "Venezuela's opposition has demanded authorities move forward on a referendum to force Nicolás Maduro from office, amid complaints that the government is digging in its heels to delay the process." Several days before protests on the issue at the headquarters of the CNE had been held after the CNE missed a deadline on announcing whether a recent petition had collected enough valid signatures. The government, in response, argued the protestors were part of a plot to topple Maduro. At the time, a poll by Venebarómetro found that "88% of 'likely' voters in a recall would choose to oust Maduro."[^cite_note-The_Guardian_2016_Maduro-362]

#### Second phase of the referendum

On August 1, 2016, the CNE announced that enough signatures had been validated for the recall process to continue. A date was not set by the CNE for the second phase to take place, which requires raising 20 percent of the electorates' signatures. While opposition leaders pushed for the recall to be held before the end of 2016, allowing a new presidential election to take place, the government vowed a recall would not occur until 2017, ensuring the current vice president would potentially come to power. [Reuters](18998750.md) reported that the government had launched 9,000 lawsuits alleging fraud in signature collection by that time.[^cite_note-Reuters_Recall_August2016-359]

On August 9, 2016, the CNE presented a timeline for the referendum that made it unlikely it would be held before the end of 2016, in part due to a new 90-day verification period for signatures.[^cite_note-Reuters_Ellsworth_2016-360][^cite_note-Bloomberg_Oust_2016-363] The second stage of the petition was estimated by the CNE to likely take place in October 2016,[^cite_note-Bloomberg_Oust_2016-363] resulting in a vote likely happening in February 2017.[^cite_note-Reuters_Ellsworth_2016-360] Opposition leaders were reported to be planning a large protest march in response,[^cite_note-Bloomberg_Oust_2016-363] with leaders accusing the CNE of favoring the incumbent Socialist Party with the wait time.[^cite_note-Reuters_Ellsworth_2016-360] According to *[Reuters](18998750.md)* on August 9, "Socialist Party leaders have dismissed the recall effort as fraudulent and noted that the elections council found nearly 10,000 signatures corresponding to people who were deceased."[^cite_note-Reuters_Ellsworth_2016-360]

Early on September 21, 2016, the National Electoral Council set new guidelines for the recall campaign that [The Associated Press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Associated_Press) described as "unfavorable to the opposition."[^cite_note-NYT_September2016-364] Among other rules, officials announced that signatures would need to be gathered from 20 percent of Venezuelan voters over three days, specifically October 26 until October 28. In addition, officials required campaigners to gather 20 percent from the electorate in each state, although "opposition leaders say they should only have to gather signatures from 20 percent of voters nationwide." The opposition, which had asked for 20,000 voting machines, was granted 5,400 by officials.[^cite_note-NYT_September2016-364] On September 21, 2016, the National Electoral Council announced the recall referendum would not be held before January 10, meaning new elections would be ruled out in favor of the VP assuming Maduro's place until the end of the term in 2019. The CNE said that the vote "could be held in the middle of the first quarter of 2017."[^cite_note-DigitalJournal_September2016-365]

#### Suspension of referendum

> When this happens there is no democracy. What Venezuela has is [dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dictatorship)...

— Jose Vicente Haro, Venezuelan law expert

[^cite_note-LAoct2016-366]

On 21 October 2016, the CNE suspended the referendum only days before preliminary signature-gatherings were to be held.[^cite_note-FOXsuspend-367] The CNE blamed alleged voter fraud as the reason for the cancellation of the referendum.[^cite_note-FOXsuspend-367]

##### Domestic reaction

Opposition leaders responded by calling on protests against the CNE's actions.[^cite_note-FOXsuspend-367] The day after the government's announcement, several thousand Venezuelans marched through Caracas protesting against the suspension.[^cite_note-REUToct2016-368] Demonstrators were led by [Lilian Tintori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lilian_Tintori) and [Patricia Gutiérrez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patricia_Guti%C3%A9rrez), wives of arrested opposition politicians.[^cite_note-REUToct2016-368]

Experts described the suspension as "unconstitutional".[^cite_note-LAoct2016-366] Venezuelan constitutional law expert Jose Vicente Haro stated that the move by the Bolivarian government shows no respect for the constitution while the [Washington Office on Latin America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Office_on_Latin_America) called the suspension "a setback for democracy".[^cite_note-LAoct2016-366]

##### International reaction

*[Reuters](18998750.md)* reported on August 4, 2016, that U.S. Secretary of State [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) had stated that "we encourage Venezuela to embrace the recall not in a delayed way that pushes it into next year, but to do this as a sign of respect for the constitution of the country and the needs of the people of the country."[^cite_note-Reuters_Kerry_Aug2016-369] On August 11, 2016, 15 countries in the [Organization of American States](22788.md) released a joint statement urging for the referendum to be held "without delay," to "contribute to the quick and effective resolution of the current political, economic and social difficulties in the country."[^cite_note-DW_ComOSA-370]

### Presidential crisis

This section is an excerpt from [Venezuelan presidential crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_presidential_crisis).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Venezuelan_presidential_crisis\&action=edit)]

The [Venezuelan presidential crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_presidential_crisis) was a political crisis concerning the leadership and legitimacy of the [president of Venezuela](3329692.md) between 2019 and 2023, with the [nation and the world divided in support](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Responses_to_the_Venezuelan_presidential_crisis) for [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) or [Juan Guaidó](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Guaid%C3%B3).

[Venezuela](32374.md) is engulfed in a political and economic crisis which has led to seven million people leaving since 2015. The process and results of the [2018 presidential elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Venezuelan_presidential_election) were widely disputed.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_BriefingNYT-371][^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_bbc.com-372] The opposition-majority [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_\(Venezuela\)) declared Maduro a usurper of the presidency on the day of his [second inauguration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_inauguration_of_Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro) and disclosed a plan to set forth its president Guaidó as the succeeding [acting president](352134.md) under article 233 of the [Venezuelan Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_Constitution).[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_bbc.com-372][^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_Rectifica-373] A week later, the [Supreme Tribunal of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Tribunal_of_Justice_\(Venezuela\)) declared that the presidency of the National Assembly was the "usurper" of authority and declared the body to be unconstitutional.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_bbc.com-372] Minutes after Maduro took the oath as president, the [Organization of American States](22788.md) (OAS) approved a resolution declaring Maduro's presidency illegitimate and urging new elections.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_VivoDebate-374] Meetings of the OAS on 24 January and the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) on 26 January were held but no consensus reached.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:8-375] During the 49th [General Assembly of the Organization of American States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Assembly_of_the_Organization_of_American_States) on 27 June, Guaidó's presidency was recognized by the organization.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_InfobaerespaldoUBN-376] Guaidó and the National Assembly declared he was acting president and swore himself in on 23 January.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_GuaidoSwears-377]

At his peak, Guaidó was [recognized as legitimate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Responses_to_the_2019_Venezuelan_presidential_crisis#RecognitionMG) by about 60 countries, despite never running as president; Maduro by about 20 countries.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_economistmoredollars-378][^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:23-379][^cite_note-380] However, Guaidó's international support waned.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_Lawler-381] Support followed geopolitical lines, with Russia, China, Cuba, Iran, Syria, and Turkey supporting Maduro, while most [Western](21208200.md) and [Latin American](18524.md) countries supported Guaidó.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_economistmoredollars-378][^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_FamiliarGeo-382][^cite_note-383] Support for Guaidó began to decline when an [uprising attempt in 2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Venezuelan_uprising_attempt) failed.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_RECOGdecline2-384][^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_bloombergtrumprevisitsv-385] Following this failure, representatives of Guaidó and Maduro began mediation.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:13-386] After a second meeting in Norway, no deal was reached.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:14-387] In July 2019, negotiations started again in [Barbados](3455.md).[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:15-388][^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:16-389][^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:17-390] In September, Guaidó announced the end of dialogue following a forty-day absence by the Maduro government as a protest against [US sanctions](77862993.md). In March 2020, the US proposed a transitional government that would exclude Maduro and Guaidó from the presidency.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:182-391] U.S. Secretary of State [Mike Pompeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Pompeo) said the US would lift sanctions if Maduro agreed to organize elections that did not include him.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:19-392] Guaidó accepted the proposal,[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:20-393] while Venezuela's foreign minister, [Jorge Arreaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_Arreaza), rejected it.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:21-394]

By January 2020, efforts by Guaidó to create a transitional government had been unsuccessful and Maduro continued to control state institutions.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_DWthousandsnovember-395][^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_AJnovGuaidocalls-396][^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_reutersmissedhismoment-397] In January 2021, the [European Union](9317.md) stopped recognizing Guaidó as president, but still did not recognize Maduro as president;[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:24-398] the [European Parliament](9581.md) reaffirmed its recognition of Guaidó as president,[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:25-399][^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:26-400] and the EU threatened with [further sanctions](60408367.md).[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:24-398] After the announcement of regional elections in 2021, Guaidó announced a "national salvation agreement" and proposed negotiation with Maduro, with a schedule for free and fair elections, with international observers, in exchange for lifting sanctions.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:22-401]

In December 2022, three of the four main opposition political parties backed and approved a plan to dissolve the interim government and create a commission of five members to manage foreign assets, as deputies sought a united strategy ahead of the [2024 Venezuelan presidential election](60825396.md),[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:1-402][^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:3-403] stating that the interim government had failed to achieve the goals it had set.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_presidential_crisis_\:7-404]

#### 2019 Venezuelan protests

This section is an excerpt from [2019 Venezuelan protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Venezuelan_protests).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2019_Venezuelan_protests\&action=edit)]

The [2019 Venezuelan protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Venezuelan_protests) were a collection of protests that were organized, since 11 January, as a coordinated effort to remove [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) from [the presidency](3329692.md). Demonstrations began following [Maduro's controversial second inauguration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_inauguration_of_Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro), developing into [a presidential crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_presidential_crisis) between Maduro and [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_\(Venezuela\)) president [Juan Guaidó](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Guaid%C3%B3). The protests also included counter-demonstrations organized by those who support Maduro.

The protests partially resumed early in 2020, but were suspended due to the arrival of [COVID-19 to Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Venezuela).

### 2019 Venezuelan uprising attempt

This paragraph is an excerpt from [2019 Venezuelan uprising attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Venezuelan_uprising_attempt).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2019_Venezuelan_uprising_attempt\&action=edit)]

On 30 April 2019, during the [Venezuelan presidential crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_presidential_crisis), a group of several dozen military personnel[^cite_note-2019_Venezuelan_uprising_attempt_FinalPhase-405] and civilians joined [Juan Guaidó](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Guaid%C3%B3) in his call for the removal of [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) as part of what he labeled "Operation Freedom" ([Spanish](26825.md): *Operación Libertad*). Reuters reported an "uneasy peace" by the afternoon of 30 April.[^cite_note-2019_Venezuelan_uprising_attempt_FinalPhase-405] During the unrest, opposition leader [Leopoldo López](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopoldo_L%C3%B3pez) was freed from house arrest after being imprisoned for five years.[^cite_note-2019_Venezuelan_uprising_attempt_auto-406] [Manuel Cristopher Figuera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Cristopher_Figuera), the head of the [Bolivarian Intelligence Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolivarian_Intelligence_Service), denounced the Maduro government and was dismissed from his position[^cite_note-407] before going into hiding.[^cite_note-2019_Venezuelan_uprising_attempt_\:3-408] At least 25 military men who opposed Maduro sought asylum at the Brazilian embassy in Caracas.[^cite_note-409]

### Overthrow attempts

On 3 May 2020 Venezuelan security forces prevented an attempt to overthrow Maduro by armed deserters from Venezuela's security forces. The attempt, named [Operation Gideon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Gideon_\(2020\)), was organised by former United States Army Special Forces operator [Jordan Goudreau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Goudreau) and the men were trained in Colombia. Goudreau said the operation had involved 60 troops, including two former US special forces members.[^cite_note-ind040520-410][^cite_note-guardian040520-411] The Venezuelan government stated that the United States and its [Drug Enforcement Administration](146720.md) (DEA) were responsible for the operation and had support from Colombia.[^cite_note-412] [Juan Guaidó](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Guaid%C3%B3) denied involvement in the operation, however, Goudreau said that Guaidó and two political advisers had signed a contract with him for \$213 million in October 2019.[^cite_note-guardian040520-411]

### 2022 Venezuelan recall referendum project

This section is an excerpt from [2022 Venezuelan recall referendum project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Venezuelan_recall_referendum_project).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2022_Venezuelan_recall_referendum_project\&action=edit)]

On 26 January 2022, the Venezuelan opposition promoted a recall referendum against the [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) government. The [National Electoral Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Electoral_Council_\(Venezuela\)) ordered the collection of the required signatures, 20% of the electoral registry, to be carried out in a single day, the equivalent of having to collect four million signatures in twelve hours, which resulted in not reaching the required signatures, the referendum being declared inadmissible and making it impossible to summon for another recall for Maduro's second presidential term.[^cite_note-2022_Venezuelan_recall_referendum_project_elpais-413]

### 2024 Venezuelan presidential election protests

These paragraphs are an excerpt from [2024 Venezuelan presidential election protests](77454821.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_Venezuelan_presidential_election_protests\&action=edit)]

Protests followed the [2024 Venezuelan presidential election](60825396.md) on 28 July, in response to [voter fraud](60825396.md#Fraud_allegations) and [other irregularities during the election cycle](60825396.md#Conduct_and_irregularities), as part of the [2024 Venezuelan political crisis](77669383.md). The election and unrest occurred in the context of the ongoing [crisis in Venezuela](52966258.md).

Statistical analyses by multiple organizations[^cite_note-2024_Venezuelan_presidential_election_protests_CNNAfterVote-414] indicated that the election was won convincingly by [Edmundo Gonzalez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmundo_Gonz%C3%A1lez_Urrutia) but those [results have not been recognized](60825396.md#Results) by incumbent [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md); the [Democratic Unitary Platform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Unitary_Platform) (PUD), an alliance of opposition parties, released vote tallies at the precinct level indicating that González won by a wide margin, while the government-controlled [National Electoral Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Electoral_Council_\(Venezuela\)) (CNE) announced an unsubstantiated result, without any precinct-level tallies, stating Maduro won. Both candidates claimed victory, while [many countries recognized González](77467581.md) as the winner.

Demonstrations to uphold the results of the election, along with vigils for political prisoners, occurred worldwide after the July election. Spontaneous protests broke out immediately after the election, while later rallies were organized by the [Venezuelan opposition](75340539.md);[^cite_note-2024_Venezuelan_presidential_election_protests_AFPMaduroYield-415] Maduro claimed the opposition was encouraging a [coup](25914154.md) and has charged demonstraters with terrorism, while initiating an unprecedented [crackdown](#Crackdown).[^cite_note-416] Maduro's security forces have gone door-to-door seeking to arrest protesters, poll workers and members of the opposition in what Maduro has referred to as *[Operation Tun Tun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Tun_Tun)*, and armed bands of Maduro supporters known as *[colectivos](42254804.md)* have joined security forces in repressing dissent. As of 14 August 2024, at least 2,200 persons are reported to have been arrested, and 25 killed;[^cite_note-2024_Venezuelan_presidential_election_protests_HundredThousand-417] Maduro has announced plans to continue to seek the arrest of dissenters, and to rehabilitate two prisons to house those detained.

The repression has been [widely condemned](#International) by international groups; [Amnesty International](18947898.md) penned an open letter requesting urgent action from the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) (ICC),[^cite_note-2024_Venezuelan_presidential_election_protests_AIonICC-418] on the basis of an [ongoing ICC investigation](62526848.md) of possible [crimes against humanity](61099.md) under Maduro's regime.

## Capture of Maduro

Main article: [2026 United States intervention in Venezuela](82021123.md)



Nicolas Maduro aboard the [USS *Iwo Jima*](583918.md)

On January 3, 2026, U.S. forces captured Maduro and his wife, Cilia Flores, in an operation, codenamed "**Operation Absolute Resolve**". The operation involved coordinated airstrikes across northern Venezuela and a surprise pre-dawn raid by elite U.S. troops on a Caracas military compound. The couple was transported to New York City to face federal charges of narco-terrorism and drug trafficking. Many analysts have likened the operation to [Operation Nifty Package](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Nifty_Package) which led to the capture of Panamanian military leader, [Manuel Noriega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Noriega),[^cite_note-419] as well as [Operation Red Dawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Red_Dawn), which led to the capture of former Iraqi president, [Saddam Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein).[^cite_note-420]

### Aftermath

Vice President [Delcy Rodríguez](44199228.md) assumed the presidency, following the ousting of Maduro.[^cite_note-Reuters-01032026-421] In just a few months, Rodríguez reshuffled roughly 40% of her cabinet, replacing 17 ministers and updating military leadership to install her own loyalists. Powerful Maduro loyalists, such as long-serving Defense Minister ⁠[Vladimir Padrino López](45272341.md) and Attorney General [Tarek William Saab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarek_William_Saab), were stripped of their posts.[^cite_note-422][^cite_note-423] This consolidation was further highlighted when Venezuela’s Minister of Industry and National Production, [Alex Saab](61390840.md), was also captured in February 2026 in a joint operation by American and Venezuelan intelligence agencies.[^cite_note-Second_Arrest-424][^cite_note-Reuters_Second_Arrest-425] In May 2026, he was deported to the US.[^cite_note-426]
